{"candidates": ["Major Depressive Disorder", "Persistent Depressive Disorder"], "note": "The patient's description of a constant, unrelenting low mood for two years and three months, along with significant sleep disturbances and intense feelings of worthlessness and guilt, continues to align with both Major Depressive Disorder and Persistent Depressive Disorder. The key distinguishing factor between these two conditions is the duration of symptoms, with Persistent Depressive Disorder requiring a minimum of two years. Given the patient's report of symptoms lasting two years and three months without any periods of significant improvement, both diagnoses remain plausible. However, the absence of any noted periods of normal functioning or emotional relief, as typically seen in Persistent Depressive Disorder, slightly leans the diagnosis towards Major Depressive Disorder, but not enough to rule out Persistent Depressive Disorder at this stage. Further questioning is needed to fully differentiate between these two conditions and to assess for any potential specifier that might influence the diagnosis, such as psychotic features or melancholic features, though the latter seems less likely given the information provided.", "is_final": false}

Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Initial Insomnia: Difficulty falling asleep and taking a long time to do so.\n  - Middle Insomnia: Waking up frequently during sleep or having difficulty falling back asleep.\n  - Early Morning Awakening: Waking up too early and being unable to fall back asleep.\n  - Hypersomnia: Sleeping for long periods at night or excessive sleep during the day.\n  - Nightmare Disorder: Sleep disturbance caused by repeated and distressing dreams.\n  - Non-Restorative Sleep: Feeling unrefreshed despite having slept enough.\n  - Feelings of Worthlessness: A persistent perception that one is useless or inferior to others.\n  - Self-Criticism: Excessively and repeatedly blaming oneself for past minor failures or flaws.\n  - Reduced Self-Efficacy: Distrust in one's own ability to perform tasks or solve problems.\n  - Chronic Sense of Inferiority: Persistently evaluating oneself lower in comparison to others.\n  -     "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I've been feeling really down and depressed, and it just doesn't seem to lift. This constant sadness has been going on for about two years and three months now. I just feel so weary and felt like I finally needed to seek help.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about your low mood over these two years and three months, has it been consistently present or have you had periods where you felt somewhat better?\n\nPatient: It has been completely constant, without any real breaks at all. I can't remember a single time over these two years where I felt truly better. It's just a continuous, heavy sadness every single day.\n\nDoctor: How has this constant sadness affected your daily life, such as your relationships, work, or ability to take care of yourself?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to describe the impact, to be honest. I haven't really thought about it in those terms, as I've just been focused on trying to get through each day.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you still enjoy or take pleasure in activities that you used to like before this period of sadness began?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure, to be honest. I haven't really thought about whether I still enjoy my usual activities or not lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns since this period of sadness began?\n\nPatient: My sleep has been deeply affected; I struggle to fall asleep, wake up constantly, have nightmares, and even when I sleep for a long time, I always wake up feeling unrefreshed. On the other hand, I haven't really noticed any major changes in my appetite. It is mostly my sleep that is completely ruined.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any feelings of worthlessness, guilt, or recurrent thoughts of death, either about yourself or others, during this period of sadness?\n\nPatient: Yes, I feel deeply worthless and find myself constantly blaming myself for past mistakes. However, I haven't had any thoughts of death or wanting to end my life.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe the intensity of your feelings of worthlessness and guilt, and have they been present throughout the entire two years and three months, or have they varied in intensity over time?\n\nPatient: These feelings of worthlessness are very intense and feel like a constant, heavy weight that I just can't shake off. They have been present pretty much the entire two years and three months, without really changing or letting up. It just feels like a continuous loop of feeling useless every single day.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods, even brief, where these feelings of worthlessness and guilt were significantly less intense or even absent, allowing you to experience some level of normal functioning or emotional relief?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any breaks or relief at all. These feelings of worthlessness and constantly blaming myself have been completely constant throughout the entire two years and three months. I haven't experienced a single moment of normal emotional functioning or any letup from this weight.\n\nDoctor: For how long at a time can you go without feeling this overwhelming sadness and worthlessness, is it possible for you to have days or even weeks where you feel relatively okay, or is it a constant, daily experience?"

It is a constant, daily experience, and I never have days or even weeks where I feel relatively okay. These feelings of sadness and worthlessness have been with me every single day without any breaks at all. This has been going on continuously for the entire two years and three months.

{"candidates": ["Major Depressive Disorder", "Persistent Depressive Disorder"], "note": "The patient's description of a continuous, unrelenting low mood for two years and three months, along with significant symptoms such as intense feelings of worthlessness, guilt, and sleep disturbances, aligns with both Major Depressive Disorder and Persistent Depressive Disorder. The key distinguishing factor between these two conditions is the duration and consistency of symptoms. Given the patient's report of constant symptoms without any relief for over two years, both diagnoses remain plausible. However, the lack of any mention of discrete episodes of depression (which might suggest Major Depressive Disorder) and the continuous nature of the symptoms lean slightly more towards Persistent Depressive Disorder. Further questioning is needed to fully differentiate between these two diagnoses, particularly to assess if there have been any periods that could be considered discrete episodes of major depression or if the symptoms have been consistently present at the same level of severity.", "is_final": false}

Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.
